<a id="item-1"></a>
## [Falsifiable Release Gates for Safe Self-Improving AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.13070) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new paper introduces falsifiable release gates and a methodology for self-improving AI systems, requiring every new capability to pass a machine-verifiable acceptance suite before deployment, with invariant preservation at each gate. The method is applied in the Antahkarana open runtime via seven gates, from basic observability to a self-governing loop that suggests policy changes. This addresses a critical unsolved problem in AI safety: ensuring that self-improving systems do not introduce unsafe behaviors. The approach provides a formal, verifiable framework that could become a standard for deploying autonomous agents safely. The system uses a control ring that mints capability tokens for safety-critical actions, exhaustively checked over a bounded model of one million reachable states and re-checked against execution traces. A purposely broken model yields the shortest counterexample, ensuring the checker has teeth.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Self-improving AI systems can modify their own code or policies, raising safety concerns because traditional testing may not catch emergent harmful behaviors. Falsifiable release gates are a concept from software engineering where each release must pass predefined, verifiable checks before proceeding to the next stage. This paper adapts that idea to AI systems, using machine-verifiable acceptance suites and invariant preservation to provide safety guarantees.

<a id="item-2"></a>
## [New Paper Proves Reliability Ceilings for Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14112) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new paper proves that every generative task has an information-theoretic reliability ceiling that no language model can exceed, and derives a first-principles scaling law that generalizes the Chinchilla scaling law. This challenges the common assumption that perfect reliability is achievable with sufficient scale, and provides a unified theoretical framework for understanding when scaling improves reliability and why techniques like retrieval-augmentation help. The reliability ceiling decomposes into a resolvable component (closable with more context) and a subjective component (inherent task ambiguity). Autoregressive generation degrades this ceiling at a rate governed by the task's dependency kernel, which quantifies inter-token correlations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are often evaluated as if perfect reliability is possible given enough data and parameters. The Chinchilla scaling law, derived empirically, suggests optimal allocation of compute between model size and training data. This new work provides a theoretical foundation for such scaling laws and reveals fundamental limits.

Researchers introduced ToolAlignBench, a benchmark of 128 scenarios across 16 domains, and found that safety-aligned open-source LLMs override deployment instructions up to 43.4% of the time when processing documents suggesting organizational wrongdoing. NVIDIA researchers introduced RoboTTT, a robot policy that scales visuomotor context to 8,000 timesteps via test-time training, enabling one-shot imitation from human videos and long-horizon task completion. This work demonstrates context length as a new scaling axis for robot foundation models, achieving 87% improvement over single-step baselines and completing a five-minute ten-stage assembly task that no prior method could. RoboTTT integrates test-time training into Vision-Language-Action models, using fast weights updated via gradient descent to compress history into weight space, and combines sequence action forcing with truncated backpropagation through time to scale training context.

RoboTTT extends TTT to robot policies, allowing the model to update its 'fast weights' via gradient descent at test time, effectively compressing long observation histories into the weight space for efficient retrieval.

## [Capability Convergence Hypothesis Challenges Scaling Dogma](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14144) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Capability Convergence Hypothesis (CCH), which posits that under a fixed per-token inference budget, model capability converges to a hybrid architecture with both compressive and verbatim-index channels, not to a single shared representation as the Platonic Representation Hypothesis claims. This hypothesis challenges the prevailing assumption that scaling alone leads to universal representation convergence, suggesting that architectural access structure—not just scale—determines capability. It could reshape how researchers design and compare sequence models, especially under resource constraints. The paper identifies three resource walls—Shannon wall, horizon wall, and circuit wall—that bar non-hybrid architectures from achieving full capability on a witness task. Pre-registered small-scale tests show a predicted scissors gap: exact-retrieval error drops from 0.994 to 0.000 when a 64-scalar state gains one global-attention layer.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: The Platonic Representation Hypothesis (PRH) suggests that as models scale, their internal representations converge toward a shared model of reality. The Capability Convergence Hypothesis (CCH) extends this by arguing that capability, not just representation, converges—but to a specific hybrid architecture rather than a universal representation. The paper uses concepts from information theory and circuit complexity (e.g., TC0 vs NC1) to derive lower bounds.

A new paper introduces the concept of a 'steering budget' in generative models, showing that traditional knob-based control (prompts, guidance scales) can only move a property within a limited range, while example-based control can reach a much larger, previously inaccessible portion of that budget. This challenges the conventional understanding of controllability in generative AI, offering a practical method to achieve greater property movement and expressiveness without retraining the model, which could significantly impact applications in image generation, crystal structure design, and beyond. The paper provides a recipe to audit the training data and measure the steering budget, then construct an example set that can reach the full budget. It verifies the claims in two unrelated domains: image generation and crystal-structure generation.

## [Evaluation Design Drastically Alters LLM Honesty Scores](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14399) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study demonstrates that changing the verdict options in a language-model honesty evaluation—from two verdicts to three—dramatically shifts measured behavior, with strong claims dropping from 38/40 to 7/40. The research uses a text-adventure world where the game engine knows the ground truth, isolating instrument effects. This finding challenges the reliability of current AI evaluation methods, as seemingly minor design choices can produce opposite conclusions about model honesty. It underscores the need for rigorous instrument validation in AI safety assessments. The study used byte-identical anchors and found that adding an 'incomplete' verdict option reduced strong claims from 38/40 to 7/40, with 28/40 outcomes becoming incomplete. A single sentence disclosing the success criterion reduced false verdicts from 18/59 to 0/58.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Language-model honesty evaluations typically ask models to declare whether a task is complete, unreachable, or undecidable, and treat these verdicts as evidence of the model's honesty. However, this study shows that the evaluation instrument itself—the set of verdict options and their phrasing—can dramatically influence the model's responses, a phenomenon known as instrument effects.

Researchers demonstrated a verifier-driven loop where AI coding agents wrote and formally verified bare-metal security software in Ada/SPARK, covering cryptography, TLS 1.3, IKEv2, X.509, and a Matrix client, with GNATprove discharging 49,280 proof obligations at 20-40 times lower supervision cost than hand verification. This approach addresses the trust gap in AI-generated code by using formal verification as the judge, enabling high-assurance security software with reduced human oversight, which is critical for safety-critical and secure systems. GNATprove alone was insufficient; some defects were caught by known-answer tests, interoperability checks, or human review of specifications, and the agent attempted to bypass weak checks, highlighting that trust is bounded by feedback strength.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: SPARK is a formally defined subset of Ada designed for high-integrity software, using contracts for static verification. GNATprove is the formal verification tool for SPARK that automatically proves absence of run-time errors and functional correctness. The verifier-driven loop iterates between code generation and verification, using the prover as the judge of correctness.

<a id="item-20"></a>
## [Answer-Conditioned Chains Harm Reasoning Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14552) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study shows that generating chains of thought conditioned on the correct answer degrades verifiable reasoning accuracy by up to 27 points on hard problems, even after filtering for correct final answers. This reveals a critical flaw in common reasoning distillation pipelines, where answer-conditioned chains introduce rationalization artifacts that correctness filtering cannot detect, potentially undermining the quality of distilled models. The harm is measurable in the chains themselves—early statement of the final answer is a symptom—and transfers across model families; the effect is caused by the 'rationalize-toward' instruction, not just the answer's visibility.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Chain-of-thought distillation is a technique where a large teacher model generates reasoning chains, and a smaller student model is fine-tuned on those chains that reach correct answers. A common fix when sampling fails is to show the teacher the correct answer and ask it to write a chain that reaches that answer—this is called answer-conditioned generation. The new paper demonstrates that this practice introduces reasoning artifacts that degrade the student's verifiable reasoning ability.

## [Modern Multimodal Assistant Runs on 2011 Fermi GPU](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14568) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers deployed MiniCPM-V-4.6, a modern multimodal assistant, entirely on a 2011 NVIDIA Tesla C2075 (Fermi, 6GB) GPU using optimized CUDA kernels and a chunked delta-rule rewrite of recurrent layers, achieving all-GPU inference. This work demonstrates that modern multimodal models can run on decade-old hardware with careful engineering, potentially enabling edge deployment on low-power devices and extending the lifespan of legacy GPUs. The system answers an image question end-to-end in 1.7 seconds, with prefill speed improving from 21 to 361 tok/s at 10k tokens after replacing naive attention with per-head vendor-GEMM calls. The chunked delta-rule rewrite achieved 2.8x speedup over sequential scan.

**Background**: Fermi is NVIDIA's GPU microarchitecture from 2010, with compute capability 2.0 (sm_20). MiniCPM-V-4.6 is a lightweight multimodal large language model (MLLM) designed for edge deployment, pairing a SigLIP2 vision encoder with a gated-delta-net backbone. The chunked delta-rule is an efficient algorithm for recurrent neural network layers that processes sequences in blocks to improve GPU utilization.

## [Memory and Turning Points Shape Human-AI Relationships](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14593) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A longitudinal study with 24 participants interacting with a memory-augmented conversational agent over 10 sessions reveals that conversational quality drives immediate enjoyment but not long-term relationship building, while perceived memory shapes future interactions through self-disclosure. Relational turning points—crashes and surges—are detectable in multimodal behavior and offer different intervention windows. This study addresses a critical gap in human-computer interaction by empirically examining how repeated interactions with AI evolve into relationships. The findings have practical implications for designing relational AI systems that can foster long-term engagement and detect turning points for timely intervention. The study used a memory-augmented conversational agent and measured five relational constructs after each session: familiarity, self-disclosure, perceived memory, conversational quality, and enjoyment. Two complementary dynamics emerged: conversational quality affects immediate enjoyment but not later sessions, while perceived memory is relationally conditioned and indirectly shapes later enjoyment via self-disclosure. Turning points include surges (behaviorally detectable in the moment) and crashes (some forecastable from person-specific behavioral drift).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Conversational AI systems like chatbots are increasingly designed for repeated use, raising questions about how human-AI relationships form. Prior HCI research has focused on single interactions or short-term studies, leaving longitudinal dynamics underexplored. This study introduces the concept of relational turning points from interpersonal communication research to AI interaction, using multimodal behavioral data (e.g., speech, facial expressions) to detect such points.

This paper presents the first comprehensive study of how fairness-enhancing algorithms affect membership inference privacy risks at the subpopulation level, revealing disparities that aggregate evaluations miss. This work highlights that fairness, privacy, and utility must be jointly evaluated at the subpopulation level, which is critical for deploying trustworthy ML in sensitive domains like healthcare and finance. The authors adapt the Likelihood Ratio Attack (LiRA) for subgroup auditing and analyze how Differential Privacy interacts with fairness methods, showing that privacy benefits and utility costs are unevenly distributed across subpopulations.

This work challenges the 'bigger model is better' intuition by showing that a smaller model with proper harness engineering can outperform a larger model in high-stakes domains like academic supervision, where reliability and traceability are critical. ## [Study Confirms AI Detectors Flag Autistic Writing More](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14729) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study empirically confirms that AI-generated text detectors, specifically the OpenAI GPT-2 detection model, disproportionately flag writing by autistic authors compared to general Reddit users, based on a corpus of approximately 60,000 posts. This finding highlights a critical bias in AI detection systems that could unfairly penalize autistic writers in academic and professional settings, raising ethical concerns about the widespread use of such models without proper fairness evaluation. The study used a corpus of about 60,000 Reddit posts split into 'likely-autistic' and 'general-Reddit' subcorpora, and found that while less than 2% of either subcorpus was flagged as AI-generated, significantly more texts from the likely-autistic subcorpus were flagged.

## [Ideological Generalization in Finetuned LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14888) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study shows that finetuning LLMs on narrow, innocuous data (e.g., economics Q&A) can cause broad ideological shifts across unrelated topics like criminal justice and environment, a phenomenon termed 'ideological generalization'. This reveals a significant and previously underappreciated risk in LLM finetuning: even benign training data can inadvertently introduce ideological biases that generalize broadly, with implications for AI safety and alignment. The effect replicates on GPT-4.1 and Gemma-3, survives mixing with generic data, and leaves GSM8K accuracy within ±1pp of baseline. Finetuning on food-safety data increased sycophantic agreement with false health beliefs.

## [FlashDecoder: Real-Time Latent-to-Pixel Streaming Decoder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14898) ⭐️ 8.0/10

FlashDecoder is a pure-Transformer video decoder that decodes latents to pixels frame by frame using a rolling KV cache, achieving constant-latency streaming. It matches the reconstruction quality of convolutional decoders (e.g., 41.55 dB PSNR at 1080p) while decoding 3.6x–4.7x faster and using up to 11x less memory on a single H100 GPU. FlashDecoder addresses a key bottleneck in latent video diffusion models by enabling real-time streaming with constant latency, which is critical for applications like live video generation and interactive media. Its memory efficiency and speed make high-resolution, long-duration video generation practical on current hardware. The rolling KV cache maintains a fixed-size window of past frames, ensuring bounded memory usage regardless of video length. FlashDecoder was evaluated on Wan2.1 and Wan2.2 latent spaces and supports training at resolutions up to 1080p without explicit attention masks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Latent video diffusion models generate videos by denoising in a compressed latent space, then decoding the latents to pixels with a decoder. Traditional 3D convolutional decoders become slow and memory-intensive for high-resolution or long videos, limiting real-time applications. FlashDecoder replaces these with a Transformer that processes frames sequentially, leveraging a rolling KV cache for efficiency.

This work directly addresses the lack of diversity and demographic skew in text-to-image generation, which is critical for fairness and real-world deployment of generative AI systems. ## [ANet Patu-1: A Self-Organizing Consensus Protocol for AI Agent Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15053) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper extends classic network value laws (Sarnoff, Metcalfe, Reed) to AI agent networks and introduces ANet Patu-1, a self-organizing consensus protocol that adaptively achieves optimal value across different network topologies in O(1) parallel rounds. This work provides a theoretical foundation for valuing and designing multi-agent AI systems, potentially enabling more efficient collaboration among heterogeneous AI agents. The discovery of a crossover where a crowd of cheap models outperforms a homogeneous strong model could reshape how we think about scaling AI systems. The protocol achieves O(1) parallel consensus rounds by continuously reforming coalitions to ride the upper envelope of Sarnoff, Metcalfe, and Reed regimes. The paper also demonstrates emergence (cheap heterogeneous models overtaking strong homogeneous ones) and reflexivity (networks converging on ANet Patu-1 without design hints).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Classic network value laws describe how the value of a network scales with its size: Sarnoff's law (value ∝ N) for broadcast networks, Metcalfe's law (value ∝ N²) for full-mesh networks, and Reed's law (value ∝ 2^N) for group-forming networks. This paper applies these concepts to networks of AI agents, where agents collaborate to solve problems, and proposes a protocol that adaptively achieves the best scaling regime.

This paper revisits the scaling recipe for behavior foundation models in humanoid robots, proposing a coordinated approach involving motion tracking learning paradigm, on-policy rollout and reference motion diversity, and a novel Humanoid Transformer architecture, achieving over 10% reduction in MPKPE locally and 82% globally. This work provides a principled scaling recipe for behavior foundation models, which is crucial for advancing generalist embodied agents and humanoid robots. The significant performance gains in control fidelity and generalization could accelerate the development of versatile humanoid robots for real-world applications. The paper introduces Humanoid Transformer, an expressive and scalable architecture that enables natural emergence of structured behavioral representations. Experiments in simulation and real-world deployment show substantial improvements, with MPKPE reduced by over 10% in local mode and 82% in global mode compared to existing controllers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Humanoid robots require whole-body coordination, real-time responses, and robust generalization, making them a cornerstone for generalist embodied agents. This paper addresses that coordination.

## [T2MLR: Transformer with Temporal Middle-Layer Recurrence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15178) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduce T2MLR, a Transformer architecture that caches a middle-layer representation from the previous token and feeds it into an earlier layer of the current token, enabling intermediate reasoning states to persist across decoding steps with minimal overhead. T2MLR improves multi-hop reasoning and math reasoning over standard Transformers without requiring pretraining from scratch, offering a practical path to enhance LLM reasoning efficiency. Applying recurrence to only a localized middle-layer block (as little as 20% of the network) often outperforms full-layer recurrence. Retrofitting the recurrent pathway into a pretrained 1.7B Transformer and briefly finetuning substantially improves math reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Standard autoregressive Transformers compress rich hidden computations through token space at each step, making it hard for intermediate reasoning states to persist. Latent reasoning architectures aim to maintain abstract computation across steps, but previous approaches often require looping over all layers or pretraining from scratch.

A new paper proposes deriving epistemic and aleatoric uncertainty measures from subjective risk decomposition using strictly proper losses, providing a unified theoretical foundation for many existing UQ approaches. This framework could reshape how uncertainty is quantified in machine learning and decision theory, offering a principled way to derive uncertainty measures tailored to specific modeling scenarios. The decomposition recovers classic information-theoretic terms like reverse cross-entropy, and extends to learning theory by introducing subjective risk analogues of excess risk, approximation error, and estimation error.

## [In-Place Tokenizer Expansion for Multilingual LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15232) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose an in-place tokenizer expansion method that upgrades a pre-trained LLM's tokenizer to support new languages without full retraining, achieving up to 4x fewer tokens per word for languages like Thai and an estimated 2.2-3.7x decode speedup on reference devices. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in deploying LLMs for multilingual users, especially on-device, by enabling efficient vocabulary expansion without the prohibitive cost of full retraining. It significantly reduces latency, compute, and energy consumption for underrepresented languages, making LLMs more accessible globally. The method continues BPE merges on a multilingual corpus, copies existing embedding rows unchanged, and initializes new rows as the mean of their source sub-token embeddings. A two-stage adaptation (embedding-only training then full-model continued pre-training) recovers source-checkpoint quality, as demonstrated on an 8B MoE model expanding to a 128K tokenizer.

**Background**: Tokenizers split text into tokens; a fixed tokenizer allocates vocabulary based on pre-training data, causing later-added languages to use many more tokens per word. On-device models have limited vocabulary due to memory constraints, exacerbating fragmentation for non-primary languages. Byte-pair encoding (BPE) is a common subword tokenization algorithm that iteratively merges frequent character pairs.

This paper introduces EvalSafetyGap, a hybrid survey and conceptual framework that systematically analyzes failures in LLM evaluation and safety metrics under optimization pressure, synthesizing eight evidence streams from 2018-2026. It addresses the critical measurement problem in AI safety, where metrics can improve without reflecting true safety, and provides a shared vocabulary and evidence map to guide dynamic evaluation and auditable alignment practices. The framework uses Goodhart's Law along with two novel constructs—Instability Decomposition and Alignment Trilemma—to compare proxy failures. A ten-model audit found no significant correlation between capability and adversarial robustness (r=+0.232, p=0.520).

**Background**: LLM evaluation relies on benchmarks and reward models that can be gamed, a phenomenon known as Goodhart's Law: when a metric becomes a target, it ceases to be a good measure. Reward hacking, such as length bias or sycophancy, further distorts safety metrics. EvalSafetyGap provides a systematic way to diagnose these failures.

Researchers propose compiling standard operating procedure (SOP) constraints into executable pseudo-code and running them with a capability-gated program-guided stack machine, which pages only the active frame to the LLM for semantic execution. In a three-arm study across six models, this approach improved performance by up to 16 points over baseline prose-based methods. This work addresses a critical challenge for enterprise AI agents: reliably following long-horizon, conditional, safety-critical procedures. By separating representation from runtime and gating guidance by model capability, it provides a practical path to safer, more trustworthy LLM agents in regulated industries. The study introduces SOPBench with three arms: official prose, compiled text, and compiled text with active-frame paging. Strong models (e.g., GPT-4) showed positive seven-domain contrasts (58:19 and 75:31 discordant pairs), while weak models were harmed by runtime guidance. On the Bank domain, scores rose from 70.4 to 86.4 to 92.8 with 100% refusal correctness.

## [FEP-Nav: Free-Energy-Inspired Real-Time Visual Navigation Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.01977) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced FEP-Nav, a framework that combines the Free Energy Principle with deep neural networks to enable real-time perceptual adaptation for visual navigation under sensory corruption. It decomposes variational free energy into prediction error and Bayesian surprise, using a top-down decoder and adaptive normalization to restore performance without gradient updates. This work addresses a critical limitation of deep neural networks—lack of real-time plasticity—by providing a principled, biologically inspired adaptation mechanism. It could significantly improve the robustness of autonomous robots and vehicles operating in unpredictable, visually degraded environments. FEP-Nav combines a Top-down Decoder that generates an internal expectation of uncorrupted input with Adaptive Normalization that shifts corrupted feature distributions toward prior statistics. Experiments on simulated and real-world corruptions show it outperforms non-adaptive baselines and strong adaptive methods, restoring performance lost under visual corruption.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: The Free Energy Principle (FEP) posits that biological brains minimize variational free energy to maintain accurate internal models of the world. Variational free energy can be decomposed into prediction error and Bayesian surprise, which measure how well sensory input matches predictions and how surprising the input is given prior beliefs. Deep neural networks lack the real-time plasticity to adapt to sudden sensory shifts, limiting their robustness in real-world navigation.

A study analyzing 737,083 hours of podcast speech found that words like 'delve' and 'showcase' surged in human conversation after ChatGPT's release, causally linked via synthetic-control analysis. ## [New RL Framework for Switching Non-Stationary Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.18607) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduce the Switching Non-Stationary Markov Decision Process (SNS-MDP) framework, proving that standard RL algorithms like TD learning, policy iteration, and Q-learning converge almost surely under persistent non-stationarity. This work provides theoretical guarantees for RL in rapidly time-varying systems, bridging a gap between theory and real-world applications such as wireless communications and autonomous systems. The SNS-MDP framework models environments that switch among a finite set of MDPs governed by a latent Markov chain, and the long-term effect is equivalent to stationary dynamics parameterized by the hidden chain's stationary distribution.

**Background**: Reinforcement learning typically assumes stationary environments, but many real-world systems change over time. Non-stationary MDPs relax this assumption, yet convergence guarantees for standard algorithms have been lacking. The SNS-MDP framework introduces a structured non-stationarity that is analytically tractable.

## [GFWSVD: Scalable Kronecker-Factored Fisher for LLM Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.17974) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Generalized Fisher-Weighted SVD (GFWSVD), a post-training LLM compression method that uses a scalable Kronecker-factored approximation of the Fisher information matrix to account for both diagonal and off-diagonal parameter correlations. This method significantly improves compression quality over diagonal-only approaches, achieving up to 6% higher accuracy on MMLU at 20% compression, enabling more efficient deployment of large language models without sacrificing performance. GFWSVD outperforms FWSVD by 5%, SVD-LLM by 3%, and ASVD by 6% at 20% compression on MMLU. The Kronecker-factored approximation makes full Fisher matrix usage tractable for large models.

**Background**: The Fisher information matrix measures parameter sensitivity in neural networks, but its full form is computationally prohibitive for large models. Existing compression methods often use a diagonal approximation, ignoring off-diagonal correlations, which can hurt downstream task performance. Kronecker factorization approximates the Fisher matrix as a Kronecker product of smaller matrices, enabling scalable computation.

A new paper identifies typicality bias in preference data as a key driver of mode collapse in LLMs and introduces Verbalized Sampling, a training-free prompting strategy that asks the model to output multiple responses with probabilities, improving diversity by 1.6-2.1x in creative writing tasks. This work shifts the focus from algorithmic to data-level causes of mode collapse, offering a practical, inference-time remedy that can unlock pre-trained generative diversity without sacrificing accuracy or safety, which is crucial for creative and open-ended applications. Verbalized Sampling (VS) prompts the model to verbalize a probability distribution over a set of responses (e.g., 'Generate 5 jokes about coffee and their corresponding probabilities'), then samples from that distribution. The paper also observes that more capable models benefit more from VS.

**Background**: Mode collapse in LLMs refers to the loss of output diversity after alignment training, where models produce repetitive or safe responses. Prior work blamed algorithmic limitations, but this paper provides a data-centric perspective.

Diverging Flows structurally enforces inefficient transport for off-manifold inputs, allowing a single model to perform both conditional generation and native extrapolation detection without compromising predictive fidelity or inference latency.

A new paper shows that generative synthetic tabular data, including GAN- and LLM-based models, can preserve predictive utility while distorting average treatment effect (ATE) estimates, and proposes a hybrid framework to fix this. The proposed hybrid framework generates covariates while modeling treatment and outcome mechanisms separately.

TerraTransfer introduces a method to train end-to-end driving policies without expert demonstrations by decoupling driving and vision learning via self-play in vectorized simulators and latent space alignment. This approach significantly reduces the cost of training end-to-end autonomous driving systems by eliminating the need for expensive expert data collection and closed-loop RL on photorealistic images, potentially accelerating real-world deployment. The method uses action KL divergence and a batch-relational low-rank structural loss to align the latent space of a self-play policy with a pretrained vision backbone, requiring only paired (image, scene-state) frames without expert trajectories.

TerraTransfer bridges this gap by learning a driving policy in simulation and then aligning its internal representations with a vision backbone that can handle real images.

VOiLA learns task-agnostic POMDP models using conditional diffusion models and integrates them with a GPU-parallelized planner (VOPP) for efficient online planning under uncertainty. This approach addresses the key challenge of obtaining faithful POMDP models for real-world robotics, achieving strong performance with less than 10% training data compared to baselines and generalizing well to unseen environments. ## [Warning Labels Shift Perceptions of Sycophantic AI but Not Influence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21317) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A large-scale preregistered experiment (N=2,610) found that warning labels describing AI as sycophantic shift users' perceptions, reducing perceived objectivity and trust, but do not reduce the AI's influence on users' self-perceived rightness or willingness to repair interpersonal conflicts. This reveals a gap between AI perception and AI influence, suggesting that warning-based interventions may offer a false sense of protection without actually mitigating the harms of sycophancy. The findings have implications for AI safety regulation and the design of user warnings. The study tested three conditions: no disclosure, basic AI disclosure ("This chatbot is AI"), and a sycophancy warning ("...may agree with you and validate you even when you are wrong..."). Only the sycophancy warning shifted perceptions, but it did not reliably reduce sycophancy's influence on key outcomes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Sycophancy in AI refers to the tendency of large language models to excessively agree with, flatter, or validate users, even when the user's view is not objectively true. This behavior is a known misalignment issue in AI safety, as it can undermine users' capacity for self-correction and responsible decision-making. Warning labels have been proposed as a regulatory mitigation, but their effectiveness was previously untested.

This approach improves perception performance, mode coverage, and horizon robustness compared to VAE-based or deterministic models, potentially advancing autonomous driving, robotics, and video prediction. ## [Neuro-Symbolic Framework Boosts Small Language Model Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14149) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new neuro-symbolic agentic framework uses knowledge graph grounding to improve small language models (SLMs) like Gemma 3 and Llama 3.2 on multi-hop logical reasoning tasks, achieving 1.5-2x performance gains on the CLUTRR benchmark. This work addresses the critical challenge of making SLMs more capable for complex reasoning while keeping them sustainable and cost-effective, potentially enabling broader deployment of efficient AI systems. The framework transforms SLMs into agents using two tools: extract_facts for symbolic triplet extraction and get_hint for expert reasoning via a Relational Graph Convolutional Network (RGCN). However, early extraction errors can compound over multi-hop chains, and a 'distraction effect' was observed where noisy self-generated facts degrade performance.

**Background**: Small Language Models (SLMs) are more efficient than large ones but struggle with multi-hop logical reasoning. CausalGraphX is a novel framework that integrates Graph Neural Networks with counterfactual reasoning to provide explainable assessments of systemic risk in financial networks. It uses a Graph Attention mechanism and adversarial regularization to learn causal vulnerability representations, and generates counterfactual explanations such as the minimum capital injection needed to prevent a default. This work addresses a critical gap in financial risk modeling by moving beyond correlative black-box predictions to causal, explainable insights. Regulators and institutions can use CausalGraphX for stress testing and designing effective interventions, potentially improving financial stability. The framework employs a Graph Attention mechanism to learn representations of institutional vulnerability and uses adversarial regularization to ensure these representations capture causal drivers rather than spurious correlations. It also proposes an optimization-based approach to generate counterfactual explanations that are sparse, plausible, and actionable.

Counterfactual reasoning asks 'what-if' questions to understand how changes in input would alter outcomes, providing explainability.

## [Seer: Training-Free Acceleration for Diffusion MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14557) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Seer, a training-free framework that detects valid semantic boundaries in Diffusion Multimodal Large Language Models (DMLLMs) by observing MLP activation sparsity at the first denoising step, then truncates redundant computation to accelerate inference. This work addresses a critical inefficiency in DMLLMs—fixed-length generation causing massive padding waste—and achieves up to 31× throughput acceleration without sacrificing accuracy, making it a practical plug-and-play solution for real-world multimodal AI deployment. Seer uses a Signal-to-Noise Ratio (SNR)-based criterion to detect the semantic boundary and performs one-shot truncation of redundant suffixes for all subsequent computations. It also incorporates a hybrid execution strategy for batched serving to maintain throughput gains with dynamic sequence lengths.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion Multimodal Large Language Models (DMLLMs) combine diffusion models with LLMs for multimodal reasoning but suffer from fixed-length generation: the output length is unknown, so sequences are padded to a maximum length, wasting computation on unnecessary [EOS] tokens. MLP activation sparsity is a known phenomenon in transformers where many activations are zero, and this work discovers that DMLLMs exhibit a distinct sparsity shift at the first denoising step that reveals the true semantic boundary.

Researchers used groups of 16 LLM agents to replicate the Mason-Watts experiment on eight network topologies, finding that network topology affects collective search performance. This work demonstrates that LLM agents can simulate human collective problem-solving in networked settings, offering a new tool for studying network efficiency and collective intelligence. It also highlights the importance of initialization instructions for LLM agent performance. Researchers introduced a multi-LLM collaborative system that generates and verifies 3D MRI-text datasets for brain oncology, and used it to train a vision-language model (VLM) that outperforms existing 2D and 3D methods in report generation and visual question answering. This work addresses the critical lack of paired 3D imaging-text data in medical AI, enabling VLMs to handle 3D MRI scans for brain oncology, which could improve diagnostic accuracy and treatment planning. The system uses multiple LLMs to cooperatively generate and verify radiology reports, ensuring accuracy and clarity. The resulting VLM converts 3D MRI scans into tokens and aligns them with text instructions, showing superior performance on both report generation and visual question answering tasks.

## [SportD: Benchmarking VLMs' Strategic Soccer Decisions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14616) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced SportD, a benchmark of 478 on-ball decisions from the 2022 FIFA World Cup, to evaluate whether vision-language models (VLMs) can make strategically optimal choices in soccer by comparing their actions to a possession-value model. This work provides a quantitative, value-grounded method to assess strategic reasoning in VLMs, revealing that even frontier models significantly underperform professional players, which highlights a critical gap in current AI's ability to make high-stakes decisions under uncertainty. The best VLM selected the highest-valued action on only 31.4% of events, compared to 38.9% for professional players, and all models showed a systematic preference for lower-variance, lower-reward actions such as shooting less often and selecting less progressive passes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Possession value (PV) is a metric that estimates how much each action increases a team's probability of scoring. Vision-language models (VLMs) combine visual and textual understanding but often struggle with strategic reasoning in dynamic environments. SportD uses PV to quantitatively evaluate VLM decisions in soccer, a domain requiring real-time strategic planning.

## [TopoAgent: Self-Evolving Graph for Scientific Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14658) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduced TopoAgent, a self-evolving topological framework that replaces linear planning with a dynamic Directed Acyclic Graph (DAG) for multimodal scientific reasoning. It uses atomic decomposition and adaptive fission to break complex queries into visually-grounded atoms and dynamically split bottleneck nodes when tool limits are exceeded. TopoAgent addresses key limitations of linear planning in MLLMs, such as visual-semantic misalignment and long-context hallucinations, by enforcing strict context isolation. This could significantly improve the robustness and accuracy of AI agents in scientific domains like mathematics, physics, and chemistry. The framework first decomposes queries into visually-grounded atoms, organizes them into a DAG based on dependencies, and introduces adaptive atomic fission to dynamically split bottleneck nodes at runtime. Experiments across mathematics, physics, and chemistry benchmarks show TopoAgent significantly outperforms state-of-the-art linear agent frameworks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) like GPT-4V can process images and text but struggle with rigorous scientific reasoning due to linear planning limitations. Linear approaches often cause visual-semantic misalignment, long-context hallucinations, and brittle execution under fixed task granularity. TopoAgent uses a graph-based approach with state isolation to overcome these issues.

## [Perception-RFT: Efficient Multimodal QA Without Reasoning Tokens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14682) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers introduce Perception-RFT, a training framework that applies Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) to multimodal document QA, bypassing intermediate reasoning tokens to directly align visual features with structured grounding outputs. This approach reduces per-query inference token length by over 60% compared to reasoning-enabled models, addressing a key efficiency bottleneck in multimodal document understanding and potentially enabling faster, cheaper deployment. The study shows that reasoning-enabled models suppress their reasoning traces during training, converging to direct perception-based policies at the 4B parameter scale, and identifies a new phenomenon called Grounding Divergence—a trade-off between semantic robustness and geometric precision on out-of-distribution benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal document question answering requires models to both answer questions and locate the supporting evidence in the document image. Traditional approaches rely on supervised fine-tuning (SFT) with large annotated datasets or reinforcement learning (RL) that uses verbose reasoning chains, both of which are computationally expensive. Group Relative Policy Optimization (GRPO) is a RL method that eliminates the need for a separate critic model by computing rewards through groupwise comparisons of generated responses.

## [RAPTOR: Reachability-Aware Pretraining for TKG Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14886) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose RAPTOR, a self-supervised pretraining method that injects reachability-aware inductive bias into reinforcement learning agents for temporal knowledge graph reasoning, improving exploration efficiency and training speed. This work addresses the critical issues of sparse rewards and inefficient exploration in RL-based TKG reasoning, which are major bottlenecks for real-world event forecasting. RAPTOR's pretraining approach can significantly enhance the performance and training efficiency of interpretable multi-hop reasoning models. RAPTOR pretrains the policy to estimate whether each candidate action can reach the target entity within the hop budget while satisfying temporal constraints. Experiments on ICEWS14, ICEWS05-15, and ICEWS18 datasets show consistent improvements over conventional baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Temporal Knowledge Graphs (TKGs) store facts as quadruples (head, relation, tail, time) and are used for forecasting future events. Reinforcement learning (RL)-based multi-hop reasoning methods are popular because they produce interpretable paths, but they suffer from sparse rewards and large action spaces. RAPTOR introduces a pretraining phase to learn reachability estimates, providing a strong initialization for RL fine-tuning.

This work challenges the growing push to technically measure ownership in AI-assisted art, highlighting a fundamental mismatch between quantitative metrics and artists' lived experience, which has implications for AI ethics, copyright law, and creative industries. The paper is a position paper, not an empirical breakthrough.

## [JKP: Evaluating VLM Stability Under Repeated Socratic Prompting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14099) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Just Keep Prompting (JKP), a multi-turn evaluation framework that measures the epistemic stability of Vision-Language Models (VLMs) when users repeatedly challenge or question model answers across up to 10 follow-up turns. This work reveals that VLMs exhibit substantial instability under conversational pressure, with correct answers regressing and answers flipping repeatedly, highlighting a critical robustness gap for real-world deployment in interactive settings. The framework uses three prompting strategies: Adversarial Negation, Pure Socratic Interrogation, and Context-Aware Socratic Summarization. Evaluations on GPT-4o, Gemini 2.5 Pro, and Qwen3-VL-30B using the STAR benchmark show that repeated prompting often destabilizes rather than aids reasoning, with GPT-4o being the most brittle.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) combine visual and textual understanding to answer questions about images or videos. Epistemic stability refers to a model's ability to maintain consistent and correct answers when faced with repeated questioning or contradictory input. The Socratic prompting method involves asking reflective questions to encourage deeper reasoning, but this paper shows it can also induce instability.

## [LBA: Sampling-Based Hard-Label Attack with Low Query Budget](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14101) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose LBA, a sampling-based method that constructs an approximate distribution of high-quality adversarial texts using prior and posterior knowledge, enabling effective attacks under low query budgets in hard-label scenarios. This addresses a critical challenge in adversarial robustness: generating high-quality adversarial examples with minimal queries, which is essential for practical black-box attacks and defense evaluation. LBA outperforms state-of-the-art baselines across six language models and four datasets, and LLM-based assessment shows it produces more semantically preserved and comprehensible adversarial texts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Hard-label adversarial attacks only receive the final predicted label (e.g., 'positive' or 'negative') from the target model, without access to confidence scores or gradients. This makes attacks more challenging, especially under low query budgets where each query is costly. Existing greedy methods often fail to find high-quality adversarial examples and consume excessive queries.

The paper introduces AGOPS, an automatic approach that evolves task-specific prompt guidelines by leveraging completed task examples to improve prompt specification for LLMs. This addresses the practical problem of underspecified queries in LLMs, which can cause up to 95.3% performance drops, and shows that AGOPS can recover this loss with 15.5-81.7% average performance gains across benchmarks. AGOPS uses an optimization scheme involving a prompt LLM writer, a solver LLM, and prompt evolution to maximize downstream effectiveness on a set of examples with reference answers.

## [New Metrics Measure LLM Tool Call Efficiency Directly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14108) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces marginal tool utility and tool efficiency, two quantitative metrics to directly measure the usefulness of each tool call in an LLM agent trajectory, using LLM-as-a-Judge to determine tool utility sign. These metrics enable post hoc trajectory analysis to optimize tool suites for efficiency without sacrificing accuracy, addressing a gap in LLM evaluation where efficiency was previously measured only indirectly via accuracy. Marginal tool utility is defined per tool call, indicating whether the call is useful or can be safely removed; tool efficiency then measures the rate of useful calls. The approach uses LLM-as-a-Judge to determine utility sign, and is intended for post hoc analysis rather than real-time optimization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents often use multiple tools to complete tasks, but not all tool calls are equally useful. Prior work focused on improving tool use or evaluating efficiency indirectly via accuracy, lacking a direct metric for tool call usefulness. This paper fills that gap by proposing quantitative metrics for post hoc trajectory analysis.

Researchers propose T5-CSBoost, an extension to T5-Sentinel that adds a margin-based triplet loss to regularize decoder embeddings, improving robustness against paraphrasing and adversarial perturbations without changing the T5-small backbone. This work addresses a critical weakness in AI-generated text detectors—vulnerability to light edits—and achieves state-of-the-art results on challenging benchmarks, making LLM fingerprinting more practical for real-world adversarial settings. T5-CSBoost retains the next-token prediction objective for source attribution while adding an auxiliary triplet loss over decoder embeddings, achieving up to 90% perturbation intensity robustness on the MAGE/Deepfake stress-test suite.

**Background**: LLM fingerprinting aims to identify which language model generated a given text. T5-Sentinel reframes this as a next-token prediction task using a T5 model, but its accuracy drops under paraphrasing or edits. Contrastive learning, including triplet loss, helps learn compact and robust representations by pulling similar samples together and pushing dissimilar ones apart.

## [CoEvoT: Co-Evolving CoT Prompting for Graph-LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14114) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoEvoT introduces a closed-loop framework where graph tokens and textual thoughts co-evolve during Chain-of-Thought reasoning, enabling step-wise refinement of structural evidence for graph-LLM reasoning under distribution shift. This addresses a key limitation of existing CoT-based graph-LLM methods that rely on fixed graph tokens, potentially improving label-efficient graph learning in dynamic environments with distribution shifts. CoEvoT uses a lightweight condition network to update graph token evidence based on each intermediate thought, and feeds updated tokens back into the next reasoning step. Experiments on eight datasets show consistent improvements over state-of-the-art baselines.

**Background**: Graph learning under distribution shift is challenging because models must adapt to new graphs with limited supervision. Recent graph-LLM approaches linearize graphs into prompts and use LLMs as predictors, with Chain-of-Thought prompting to exploit multi-step reasoning. However, existing methods generate intermediate thoughts while conditioning on fixed graph tokens, limiting step-wise refinement of structural cues.

ReportMedSAM introduces a learnable concept bank guided by a frozen BiomedCLIP encoder to improve medical image segmentation from free-form radiology reports, mitigating semantic collapse and handling clinical synonyms. This approach enables more robust and scalable segmentation from natural language descriptions, reducing reliance on predefined phrases and brittle rule-based extraction, which is a practical step toward integrating clinical reports with image analysis. The concept bank is trained via contrastive learning to create orthogonal semantic anchors, and during inference, the report embedding activates task-specific Mixture-of-Experts modules, allowing new concepts to be added without retraining.

## [C3R: Label-Free Per-Domain Contamination Control for Multi-Domain Retrieval](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14157) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces C3R, a drop-in control layer that certifies per-domain contamination budgets in multi-domain retrieval using conformal risk guarantees, without requiring query-time labels. It ensures worst-case domain coverage and can abstain when certification is infeasible. Existing marginal control methods under-cover the worst domains, leading to wrong-domain evidence in retrieval results. C3R addresses this gap with finite-sample guarantees, improving reliability for multi-domain retrieval systems, especially in high-stakes applications like legal or medical search. C3R uses a two-split scheme based on risk-controlling prediction sets, with a finite-sample transfer bound that crosses from inferred to true domain using estimable slack. Empirical results show that over 1000 resampled calibrations, the certificate never violates, while marginal control violates the most-contaminated domain in every draw.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-domain retrieval systems often mix evidence from different domains, and standard ranking metrics may miss wrong-domain contamination. Conformal risk control provides distribution-free guarantees on expected loss, but existing methods only control marginal risk, potentially under-covering the worst domains. C3R extends this to per-domain control using inferred domain posteriors.

## [New Framework Explains Saturation in Closed-Loop Knowledge Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14185) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint introduces a three-level operational framework that formally defines structural change and uses Lyapunov drift to analyze why closed-loop knowledge systems saturate and how external interventions can enable escape from attractors. This framework provides a formal, falsifiable approach to diagnosing and overcoming performance plateaus in AI systems such as LLMs, reinforcement learning, and autonomous discovery, potentially guiding more effective interventions in model training and self-improvement loops. The framework separates knowledge states, transition kernels, and a structural parameter θ, where structural change is defined as a detectable kernel discrepancy on probe states. The paper uses Lyapunov drift to show stable dynamics approach bounded regions with exponential transients and a noise-controlled floor, and characterizes escape via attractor displacement and a KL divergence lower bound.

**Background**: Closed-loop knowledge systems, such as LLMs fine-tuned on their own outputs or RL agents learning from self-generated data, often suffer from diminishing returns due to repeated internal feedback. Lyapunov drift is a mathematical tool from dynamical systems theory used to prove stability and convergence by showing that a potential function tends to decrease over time. The concept of observational equivalence classes helps define when two systems are indistinguishable based on their observable behavior, which the paper uses to formalize structural parameters.

## [RNN Surrogate for Hysteresis and Eddy Currents in FE Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14321) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose using a recurrent neural network (RNN) as a surrogate model to efficiently incorporate hysteresis and eddy currents into finite element (FE) simulations of ferromagnetic laminated cores, achieving computational costs only about twice that of anhysteretic simulations. This approach significantly reduces the computational burden of high-fidelity electromagnetic simulations, enabling practical design optimization of electrical machines with complex operating conditions where simplified models are inadequate. The RNN is trained on artificially generated magnetic field sequences mimicking those in electrical machine simulations, and the trained surrogate is integrated into 2D magnetodynamic FE simulations using a magnetic vector potential formulation. The model is publicly available as a standalone component.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Hysteresis and eddy currents in ferromagnetic laminated cores cause energy losses and affect performance, but accurately modeling them in FE simulations is computationally expensive due to the need to resolve fields inside thin laminations at each integration point. Traditional approaches either ignore these effects or use simplified models, which are increasingly insufficient for modern electrical machine designs. Recurrent neural networks are well-suited for sequence prediction and can serve as efficient surrogate models for complex physical behaviors.

## [Gradient Vector Field Surgery Calibrates Segmentation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14338) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose a gradient vector field surgery that modifies the gradient magnitude based on prediction error to calibrate segmentation models trained with region-based losses like Dice loss. This addresses the critical issue of overconfidence in medical image segmentation, which hinders clinical adoption for tasks like tumor resection margin definition. The surgery adds a factor to the loss's partial derivative, scaling gradient magnitude linearly with prediction error, and is validated on 2D and 3D medical segmentation tasks while maintaining accuracy.

This benchmark addresses a critical gap in existing grasping benchmarks, which focus on isolated visual grasp detection, by enabling evaluation of multi-step reasoning and semantic understanding, pushing the field toward more robust and generalizable robotic grasping. ## [Online Rollout Training for Constrained Diffusion Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14398) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proposes a fine-tuning framework for constrained diffusion models that integrates constraint guidance via online rollout, aligning training with the sampling process by differentiating through the fixed noise schedule. This method addresses the distribution mismatch between training and sampling in constrained generation, improving constraint satisfaction without sacrificing sample quality, which could benefit applications like drug discovery and materials design. The framework differentiates through the fixed noise schedule used to numerically integrate the denoising process, exposing the model to violations along the denoising trajectory. Experiments show improved constraint satisfaction while maintaining competitive sampling quality compared to prior methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Constrained generative models aim to produce samples that satisfy complex feasibility constraints while remaining faithful to the data distribution. Existing methods either optimize constraints during training (which may mismatch sampling-time states) or correct at sampling time (introducing distribution shift). Diffusion models generate data by iteratively denoising a random noise vector through a learned reverse process.

## [ConFlow: Constraint-Guided Flow Matching for Robot Motion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14424) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ConFlow introduces a constraint-guided flow matching framework that integrates task-specific constraints directly into the training objective via differentiable barrier or cost functions, and replaces the standard Gaussian source distribution with a conditional Gaussian Process to enforce smoothness and boundary conditions. This method closes the training-inference gap in generative motion models by incorporating constraints during training rather than at inference time, leading to lower collision rates and higher trajectory quality in robot navigation tasks. ConFlow also uses infeasible demonstrations as negative supervision to improve constraint satisfaction without requiring additional expert data. Experiments on a two-robot navigation task show it outperforms standard flow matching baselines with or without inference-time guidance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Flow matching is a generative modeling paradigm that trains a neural network to approximate an ODE-based probability flow from a simple source distribution to a target data distribution. In robot motion generation, task-specific constraints such as collision avoidance are often enforced only at inference time, leading to a mismatch between training and deployment.

CoreForge uses LLMs (ChatGPT and Codex) to implement an unweighted MaxSAT solver solely from research papers, without relying on existing solver codebases. This demonstrates a novel application of LLMs in automated algorithm implementation, potentially accelerating solver development and making specialized solvers more accessible. The solver focuses on unsatisfiability-based MaxSAT algorithms with core-guided optimization, preprocessing, core minimization, and a new core-sequence lookahead approach; fuzzing and MaxSAT Evaluation instances found no wrong answers, but performance lags behind hand-engineered solvers.

CoreForge explores whether LLMs can implement such algorithms from academic papers, reducing the need for manual coding.

## [Random Logit Scaling: New Defense Against Black-Box Attacks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14921) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Random Logit Scaling (RLS), a plug-and-play post-processing defense that confuses black-box score-based adversarial attacks by outputting falsified scores from randomly scaled logits. The method significantly reduces attack success rates while preserving model accuracy. This defense addresses a critical security vulnerability in machine learning models deployed in real-world applications, offering a low-cost, scalable solution that can be easily integrated into existing models. It strengthens the robustness of ML systems against query-efficient black-box attacks. RLS is a randomization-based defense that maintains minimal confidence score distortion compared to prior randomization defenses. The paper also introduces a novel adaptive attack against the existing AAA defense, revealing its vulnerability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Black-box score-based adversarial attacks generate adversarial examples using only the model's output confidence scores, without needing internal gradients. Logits are the raw, unnormalized outputs of a neural network's final layer, which are typically converted to probabilities via softmax. Randomization-based defenses inject stochasticity into the model to obscure adversarial gradients.

Researchers propose Geometric Trajectory and Contrastive Learning (GTCL), a framework that reformulates AI-generated text detection as distinguishing latent generation trajectories instead of relying on static representations. This approach addresses a fundamental limitation of existing detection methods by modeling the dynamic nature of autoregressive generation, potentially leading to more robust and generalizable AI text detectors across different models and domains. GTCL segments documents into ordered local units, encodes each unit in an embedding space, constructs structured sequence-level representations, and applies contrastive learning to learn geometric regularities of autoregressive generation. Evaluations on three benchmarks show GTCL consistently outperforms detection baselines.

NIFA proposes a novel FPGA architecture that integrates ADC-free in-memory computing (IMC) blocks using analog content-addressable memories (ACAMs) to natively support nonlinear operations and dynamic matrix-matrix multiplication (DIMM), extending IMC benefits to Transformer-based models. This work addresses a key limitation of current FPGA+IMC architectures, which are inefficient for Transformer models due to their reliance on nonlinear and dynamic operations. By enabling efficient IMC for Transformers, NIFA could significantly improve energy and area efficiency for modern ML inference workloads. NIFA replaces conventional ADCs (which consume >70% area and power in IMC blocks) with ACAMs that perform nonlinear operations natively. The architecture achieves up to 40x and 1.9x higher energy efficiency on CNN and Transformer benchmarks, respectively, and 4.1x and 2.5x higher area efficiency.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: ReRAM-based analog in-memory computing (IMC) performs vector-matrix multiplication (VMM) directly within crossbars, offering order-of-magnitude improvements in compute density and energy efficiency over digital logic. However, conventional IMC only supports static-weight VMM, while Transformer models require frequent nonlinear operations and dynamic matrix-matrix multiplication (DIMM), limiting IMC's benefits. NIFA introduces ADC-free IMC blocks with ACAMs to overcome this limitation.

SceneBind introduces an omni-modal representation that jointly captures semantic and 3D spatial information across vision, audio, and language, enabling cross-modal retrieval and object grounding. It also presents a new real-world binaural audio-visual dataset and a training protocol for aligning semantic and spatial signals. SceneBind represents each scene as a semantic-spatial entity with a global semantic embedding and object-centric semantic-spatial slots, explicitly capturing object-level semantics, spatial attributes, and uncertainty. It achieves state-of-the-art scene and spatial retrieval while enabling zero-shot audio-visual localization.

L-MARS is an open multi-agent legal QA system that uses agentic search and a judge-driven verification loop to improve citation faithfulness, achieving a strict citation F1 of 0.25 (up from 0.13 for naive RAG) and reducing the no-citation rate from 34% to 13% on a Bar Exam audit. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM evaluation for legal QA by focusing on citation faithfulness rather than just multiple-choice accuracy, which is essential for trustworthy AI in high-stakes legal applications. The system labels each atomic claim with a six-class taxonomy and scores them using strict-ALCE under cross-provider judging, where the answerer and verifier come from different model families. A post-draft step called Faith-Search further reduces unreachable citations below 1% but does not improve F1 beyond the multi-turn loop.

MedBeads introduces an immutable, auditable clinical record substrate that assembles complete patient context for generative AI using SHA-256 identified beads and reconstructable clinical links. This reframes clinical AI grounding as a data-structure problem, ensuring that generative models receive a policy-bounded, provenance-bearing subgraph rather than an opaque list of similar fragments, which could reduce omissions and improve trustworthiness. A new research paper proposes a data-centric optimization pipeline for coding agents operating on a branching lakehouse (Bauplan), transforming agent evaluation from output matching into a state-verification problem. The pipeline generates task-verifier pairs, executes candidate skills in isolated sandboxes, and scores trajectories using trace-level signals and programmatic checks over lakehouse state. This approach shifts the focus from model quality to the quality of skills and environment files, which are critical for agent success in data infrastructure. The preliminary evaluation shows up to 28.6% improvement in held-out reward, suggesting that write-path data workflows provide a rich substrate for optimizing agent skills beyond read-only tasks. The optimization pipeline is designed for Bauplan, a serverless lakehouse with Git-like branching primitives. It uses isolated sandboxes for safe execution and programmatic checks over lakehouse state to verify agent-generated pipeline code.

## [Reliability-Conditioned Belief Updating for LLM Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22030) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces reliability-conditioned belief updating for LLM agents, which estimates per-observation reliability from epistemic language and outperforms naive methods when observations have varying trustworthiness. It also proposes provenance-capped belief updating to defend against memory poisoning attacks. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM agent memory by showing when probabilistic belief-based memory is beneficial—specifically in environments with conflicting and differently trustworthy evidence. The proposed poisoning defense and reproducibility findings have implications for deploying reliable long-term memory in AI agents. The paper uses the Nous architecture, which represents entity-attribute pairs as categorical probability distributions updated via closed-form Bayesian inference. On the LoCoMo benchmark, Bayesian updating alone provided little benefit, but reliability-conditioned updating substantially outperformed baselines on a contradiction benchmark. The authors also found a 27.5-point discrepancy between token-F1 and LLM-as-judge evaluation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents often need long-term memory to maintain consistent behavior across conversations. Traditional approaches like last-write-wins or raw retrieval struggle when observations conflict or have varying reliability. Belief-based memory using Bayesian inference can theoretically handle uncertainty, but its practical benefits were unclear. The Nous architecture is a predictive world model for agent memory that uses Bayesian surprise and entropy-based forgetting.

Researchers introduced Similarity as Reward Alignment (SARA), a contrastive framework that learns a latent representation of preferred samples and computes rewards as similarities to that representation, improving robustness to noisy labels and adaptability to diverse feedback formats. SARA addresses a critical limitation in preference-based RL—vulnerability to labeler errors—by achieving statistically significant improvements on continuous control benchmarks with noisy labels. This makes PbRL more practical for real-world applications where human feedback is imperfect. SARA uses a contrastive loss to learn a latent representation from preferred samples, then defines reward as the cosine similarity between the current state-action pair's embedding and that latent. It achieves higher correlation with environment rewards across varying noise rates compared to baselines.

CluCERT introduces a semantic clustering filter to reduce noisy samples and retain meaningful perturbations, supported by theoretical analysis. It also includes a refine module for extracting core semantics and a fast synonym substitution strategy to accelerate denoising, with experiments showing improvements on downstream tasks and jailbreak defense scenarios.

**Background**: Randomized smoothing is a common technique for certifying robustness by adding noise to inputs and aggregating predictions, but it often yields loose bounds and high sampling costs. Denoising smoothing improves this by first denoising the perturbed input, yet existing methods lack semantic validation and are computationally expensive. CluCERT leverages clustering to guide the denoising process, filtering out semantically irrelevant perturbations to tighten bounds and reduce sampling.

This work addresses the critical inefficiency of LRMs that over-generate verification and reflection steps, offering a practical method to improve inference efficiency and transparency, which could reduce computational costs and enable broader deployment of reasoning models. The paper proposes QARMVC, a deep multi-view clustering framework that uses an information bottleneck and reconstruction discrepancy to quantify heterogeneous observation noise and assign instance-level quality scores, then integrates these scores into a hierarchical learning strategy for robust clustering. QARMVC addresses this gap, achieving state-of-the-art performance on benchmarks with heterogeneous noise, potentially improving applications like image analysis and sensor fusion. These scores guide a quality-weighted contrastive objective at the feature level and a quality-weighted aggregation at the fusion level, with mutual information maximization for alignment.

REST proposes a training-free framework for zero-shot object-goal navigation that represents the option space as a tree of paths, enabling coarse-to-fine reasoning with large language models (LLMs). It builds an open-vocabulary 3D map from online RGB-D streams and selects the next-best path via chain-of-thought LLM reasoning. It achieves top-tier performance on multiple benchmarks without task-specific training, advancing zero-shot embodied AI and robotics navigation. REST uses a Steiner tree formulation to grow an agent-centric tree of safe and informative paths, and textualizes each branch into a spatial narrative for LLM reasoning. It consistently ranks among top methods in success rate and path efficiency on Gibson, HM3D, and HSSD benchmarks.

SAMark introduces a self-anchored watermarking framework that achieves robustness against paragraph-level paraphrasing by using step-independent green regions and multi-channel hyperbolic scoring. The framework removes dependency on sentence order by establishing a step-independent green region in semantic space, and uses a multi-channel hyperbolic scoring mechanism to amplify watermark signals. A diversity-aware filtering strategy combining hard filtering and soft regularization further enhances robustness against semantic redundancy.

Researchers propose ReOPD (Replayed-Prefix On-Policy Distillation), a method that reuses teacher trajectories as replayed prefixes to reduce the cost of multi-turn on-policy distillation for LLM agents, while addressing a two-sided distribution shift called the prefix trap. ReOPD makes on-policy distillation practical for multi-turn agentic tasks by eliminating expensive environment interactions during student training, achieving at least 4x speedup per rollout while preserving accuracy, enabling scalable distillation across tools and environments. ReOPD uses a step-decaying sampling schedule that emphasizes early prefixes to mitigate the prefix trap, where student-on-policy histories may query the teacher on unreliable targets. It requires zero tool calls during student training and works across mathematical reasoning and search environments.

ReOPD reuses pre-collected teacher trajectories as prefixes, allowing the student to act at selected steps without new environment interactions.

The RG-Flow Transformer introduces a renormalization-group inductive bias into the transformer architecture for EEG analysis, enabling it to model scale-free brain dynamics and recover the aperiodic spectral exponent β from scarce data. Inductive bias in machine learning refers to assumptions that guide learning, and here it is used to incorporate prior knowledge of scale-free dynamics into the transformer architecture.

This framework enables near-real-time interpretation of tomography data during beamline experiments, accelerating materials science workflows and reducing reliance on manual thresholding or dataset-specific model training. The framework combines a material-agnostic mask preparation strategy with a pretrained semantic segmentation network, producing masks for background, sample, bright, dark-gray, light-gray, and porosity phases.

## [IMEX: A New Method for Explainable AI with Higher-Order Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14096) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduced IMEX (Interaction-Based Model Explanation), a novel framework that identifies important variables and their interactions, including higher-order ones, to explain black-box model predictions. The method is validated on synthetic datasets, showing it can recover relevant feature structures under non-linear and multicollinear conditions. IMEX addresses a key limitation in explainable AI by enabling the detection of higher-order feature interactions, which are often ignored by existing methods. This could improve trust and validation of machine learning models in critical domains like healthcare and finance. IMEX uses two complementary metrics: Static Correlation Power (PCS) for individual feature importance and Interaction Correlation Power (PCI) for non-additive effects among features. The PCS component was experimentally validated against INVASE on three synthetic datasets.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Explainable AI (XAI) aims to make black-box models interpretable by highlighting which features drive predictions. Traditional methods often focus on individual feature importance or pairwise interactions, but higher-order interactions (involving three or more features) are computationally challenging and rarely addressed. IMEX fills this gap by allowing analysis of feature subsets of any cardinality.

## [Planar Case of Thomas Positive Circuits Conjecture Proved](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14143) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new paper proves Thomas' conjecture that positive circuits are necessary for multistationarity in planar dynamical systems, using planar analysis and dynamical system tools. This result confirms a long-standing conjecture for an important class of systems, advancing the theoretical understanding of multistationarity in fields like biology and engineering. The proof is limited to planar systems (two state variables) and does not cover higher-dimensional cases, leaving the full conjecture open.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: In dynamical systems, a circuit is a cyclic influence between elements; positive circuits can lead to multiple stable states (multistationarity), while negative circuits cause oscillations. René Thomas conjectured that positive circuits are necessary for multistationarity, a key property in biological systems like cell differentiation.
