## [ViPSAM: Visual Prompting for Medical Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.14328) ⭐️ 6.0/10

ViPSAM is a new framework that enhances the Segment Anything Model (SAM) with visual prompts from contrast-enhanced MRI to improve lesion segmentation on non-contrast CT for proton therapy planning. This work addresses a critical challenge in proton therapy planning by enabling more accurate lesion segmentation on low-contrast non-contrast CT, potentially improving treatment outcomes. It demonstrates the value of cross-modality visual prompting, which could be applied to other medical imaging tasks. ViPSAM introduces a visual prompt encoder to extract guidance features from contrast-enhanced images and a visual-guided cross-attention module to integrate non-contrast and contrast-enhanced features. The method is evaluated on liver lesion segmentation using NCCT acquired for proton therapy, outperforming representative U-Net- and SAM-based methods.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: In proton therapy planning, respiratory-gated non-contrast CT (NCCT) is commonly used for lesion segmentation, but low lesion-to-background contrast makes accurate delineation challenging. The Segment Anything Model (SAM) is a foundational model for image segmentation that shows promising zero-shot performance with prompt inputs, but adapting it to medical images, especially non-contrast modalities, remains difficult. Clinicians often reference contrast-enhanced MRI to delineate lesions on NCCT, inspiring the cross-modality visual prompting approach in ViPSAM.

## [Prompt Tuning with Adaptive Focal Loss for MCI Screening](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15047) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose a parameter-efficient prompt tuning method using frozen DINOv2-Small with three learnable prompt tokens and a MoCA-adapted adaptive focal loss for interpretable MCI screening from drawing tests, achieving an MCI-class F1 of 0.641 and AUC of 0.795. This work addresses data scarcity and class imbalance in medical imaging for early Alzheimer's detection, offering a computationally efficient alternative to full fine-tuning while providing intrinsic spatial explainability through attention maps. The method uses only 1.19 million trainable parameters, with each prompt token serving as a query in a shared cross-attention layer over image patch tokens. The MoCA-adapted focal loss integrates continuous cognitive scores into training, outperforming the heavier ResViT baseline by 0.110 in MCI-class F1.

**Background**: Mild Cognitive Impairment (MCI) is an early stage of cognitive decline that often precedes Alzheimer's disease. Automated screening from drawing tests is challenging due to data scarcity and class imbalance. Parameter-efficient prompt tuning adapts large pre-trained vision transformers like DINOv2 with minimal trainable parameters, while focal loss addresses class imbalance by focusing on hard examples.

## [Neuro-Symbolic AGI Robots with 4-Valued Logic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.09567) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a neuro-symbolic framework for AGI robots that integrates neural learning with logical deductions using Belnap's 4-valued bilattice, incorporating a closed knowledge assumption to handle unknown and inconsistent information. This work addresses key challenges in AGI, such as causality and controlled security, by combining statistical learning with formal logic, potentially enabling more trustworthy and human-like reasoning in autonomous robots. The framework uses a 4-valued truth system (true, false, unknown, inconsistent) from Belnap's bilattice, where 'unknown' represents missing knowledge and 'inconsistent' allows handling paradoxes like the Liar paradox. The closed knowledge assumption ensures that only known facts are part of the robot's knowledge base.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jul 18, 04:00

**Background**: Neuro-symbolic AI aims to combine neural networks' learning capabilities with symbolic reasoning's logical rigor. Belnap's 4-valued bilattice extends classical two-valued logic to handle incomplete and contradictory information, which is crucial for real-world AGI systems. The closed-world assumption presumes that unproven statements are false, but this paper adapts it to a 'closed knowledge' variant where unknown facts are simply absent from the knowledge base.
